(Q1) Does a GNN forecast load better than strong non-graph models? It depends on the dataset: in <sub>a</sub> t<sub>une</sub>d b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k <sub>o</sub>f 8 GNN <sub>arc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ures aga</sub>i<sub>ns</sub>t <sub>s</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>s</sub>ti<sub>ca</sub>l<sub>,</sub> b<sub>oos</sub>ti<sub>ng,</sub> f<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on-mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pers</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ence</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes, no</sub> GNN b<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>s a poo</sub>l<sub>e</sub>d <sub>gra</sub>di<sub>en</sub>t<sub>-</sub>b<sub>oos</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>on</sub> regio<sub>n</sub>s<sub>-</sub>fr<sup>1</sup><sub>,</sub> <sub>w</sub>h<sub>ereas</sub> <sub>on</sub> weave-uk <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> 2 <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> 8 GNN<sub>s</sub> h<sub>ave</sub> <sub>a</sub> l<sub>ower</sub> <sub>mean</sub> RMSE th<sub>an</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>es</sub>t b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne,</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>zero-s</sub>h<sub>o</sub>t f<sub>oun</sub>d<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l t0<sub>-gamma,</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th b<sub>y</sub> l<sub>ess</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>one</sub> <sub>see</sub>d <sub>s</sub>td<sub>.</sub>

## 4.2 Architectures and A<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation

Our benchmark covers GCN (Ki f and Wellin 2017), Gra hSAGE (SAGE) (Hamilton et al. 2017), APPNP (Gastei<sub>g</sub>er et al. 2019), GAT (Veličković et al. 2018), GATv2 (Brod<sub>y</sub> et al. 2022), Transformer-Conv (Transformer) (Shi et al. 2021), ChebConv (Deferrard et al. 2016) and TAGConv (TAG) (Du et al. 2017), all inside the same residual backbone: a linear node encoder, residual blocks with la<sub>y</sub>er <sub>norma</sub>li<sub>za</sub>ti<sub>on aroun</sub>d <sub>eac</sub>h <sub>convo</sub>l<sub>u</sub>ti<sub>on, an</sub>d <sub>a</sub> li<sub>near rea</sub>d<sub>ou</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> 48 <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>pu</sub>t<sub>s.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t<sub>s o</sub>f several seeds or architectures are combined either uniforml<sub>y</sub> or b<sub>y</sub> the ML-Pol<sub>y</sub> al<sub>g</sub>orithm (Gaillard et al. 2014), which sets the wei<sub>g</sub>hts $\hbar { j , \ i }$ � <sup>of</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>a</sup>gg<sup>re</sup>g<sup>ate</sup> $\begin{array} { r } { \widehat { y } _ { t } = \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { K } p _ { j , t } x _ { j , t } } \end{array}$ <sup>f</sup>rom <sub>p</sub>ast <sub>p</sub>er<sup>f</sup>ormance. A seed ensemble, which avera<sub>g</sub>es the forecasts of several seeds (Lakshminara<sub>y</sub>anan et al. 2017), ca<sub>p</sub>t<sub>u</sub>res the variabilit<sub>y</sub> d<sub>u</sub>e to initialization and o<sub>p</sub>timization onl<sub>y,</sub> so the s<sub>p</sub>read across seeds does not meas<sub>u</sub>re total <sub>p</sub>redicti<sub>v</sub>e <sub>u</sub>ncertaint<sub>y</sub>.

## 5.1 Datasets

regions-fr. The French network o<sub>p</sub>erator (RTE) <sub>p</sub>ublishes the half-hourl<sub>y</sub> load of the 12 French administrative re<sub>g</sub>ions, from which we use the <sub>p</sub>eriod Januar<sub>y</sub> 2015 to December 2019 and call this dataset regions-fr. Covariates are calendar variables (time of da<sub>y</sub>, weekda<sub>y</sub>, da<sub>y</sub> t<sub>yp</sub>e, holida<sub>y</sub>s, <sub>p</sub>osition in the <sub>y</sub>ear, da<sub>y</sub>li<sub>g</sub>ht savin<sub>g</sub>) and weather conditions (tem<sub>p</sub>erature, cloud cover, wind) measured at 32 stations and avera<sub>g</sub>ed within each re<sub>g</sub>ion<sub>;</sub> no weather forecast is used. The tar<sub>g</sub>et is each re<sub>g</sub>ion’s load in MW (me<sub>g</sub>awatts), and s<sub>y</sub>stem-wide scores are com<sub>p</sub>uted on their sum.

## 6<sub>.</sub>1 A<sub>rc</sub>hit<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> B<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k

Individual architectures. We then test <sub>p</sub>redictive im<sub>p</sub>ortance directl<sub>y</sub> b<sub>y</sub> retainin<sub>g</sub> ed<sub>g</sub>es accordin<sub>g</sub> to their <sub>a</sub>tt<sub>en</sub>ti<sub>on ran</sub>k <sub>an</sub>d <sub>measur</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> th<sub>e resu</sub>lti<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>orecas</sub>t <sub>error un</sub>d<sub>er a</sub> fi<sub>xe</sub>d d<sub>owns</sub>t<sub>ream mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>.</sub>
